| **Resolution** | Mirror the proven Signal #5 architecture (FP-041) with the §4.4.4-driven additions: (a) NEW entitlement-aware `PolygonForm4Fetcher` — HTTP 403 → typed `subscription_gated`, 404 → `data_unavailable`, neither throws / neither fabricates; returns BOTH `record_type='transaction'` and `record_type='holding'` rows (compute filters). (b) NEW pure compute module `compute-insider.ts` exporting `filterQualifyingTransactions` (P all; S only where `aff_10b5_one===false`; M/C/A/G excluded; first gate drops holding-only rows), `classifyRoleWeight` (3-tier title-heuristic per DEC-044 — C-suite/President 1.0, NEO-proxy via "Chief X Officer"/EVP/SVP 0.7, 10%+ owner 0.5, generic Section-16 officer 0.4, independent director 0.3; highest-applicable-weight tie-break; `(?<!vice\s)\bpresident\b` guard prevents "Executive Vice President" leaking into tier 1), and `computeInsiderSignal` (Σ shares × price × sign × role_weight × exp(-age/14) / market_cap; `null` on zero qualifying transactions OR non-positive market_cap; replay-deterministic via injected `as_of`). (c) NEW `createInsiderOrchestrator` with THREE parallel side-input fetchers per ticker (form-4 + shares-outstanding + price for market_cap), translating each entitlement-aware unavailability into the matching `SignalSkipReason`; surfaces a new `no_qualifying_transactions` skip when the filter empties the row set (the EXPECTED case for most names). (d) Cron handler `longshort-insider-compute` (verifyCronSecret + productionClock + 3-fetcher context + 3 audit events). (e) Manual sibling `longshort-insider-compute-manual` (POST + JWT + `longshort.manage` + dual audit envelope). (f) MIG-077 disarmed `job_registry` seed with daily-after-close schedule `'0 19 * * 1-5'` + `signal_registry` planned→live flip (status='live', job_registry_id, cadence note, stale_after_hours=48). (g) Extended `SignalSkipReason` with `no_qualifying_transactions` + same-commit `aggregateSkipCounts` seeding + `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` exact-match assertion update (FP-041 stale-test lesson applied). (h) Extended `JOB_ID_TO_SIGNAL_ID` + drift sentinel. signal_id locked: `'insider_transactions_90d'`. |

**Pre-build gate (operator-confirmed)** — Live probe of `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4` against the production POLYGON key returned 200 + the documented field set (`transaction_code`, `aff_10b5_one`, `transaction_shares`, `transaction_price_per_share`, `transaction_date`, `transaction_acquired_disposed`, `is_director`, `is_officer`, `is_ten_percent_owner`, `not_subject_to_section_16`, `officer_title`, `record_type`, `security_type`). **Closure** — Landed at execution commit. Signal #4 is the fourth of nine live (in the registry sense — code/migration shipped, cron disarmed). 4 live + 5 planned + 1 planned composite. Validation path: operator POST `longshort-insider-compute-manual` with `{ "as_of": "YYYY-MM-DD" }` → expected SPARSE result (most names `no_qualifying_transactions` → `is_present=0` is NORMAL, not failure; a minority carries real weighted-decay z-scores). Cron wiring per DEC-043 is a separate operator step. Authority: ACT-154.

**Addendum (ACT-155, 2026-06-09) — Market-wide Form 4 fetch (CPU-limit fix; Constitution Rule 8 supersession via addendum).** First post-deploy fire died with HTTP 546 `WORKER_RESOURCE_LIMIT` (~2 s edge-isolate CPU budget exceeded ~6 s after boot — NOT memory, NOT wall-time). Root cause: per-ticker fetch across ~839 universe tickers = ~2517 HTTPS round-trips (Form 4 + shares + price ×839) + 839 JSON parses + per-row regex/decay loops — ~3–5× short-interest's CPU footprint. Fix: replace per-ticker Form 4 calls with ONE market-wide paginated call to `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4?transaction_date.gte=…&lte=…` (NO `ticker=`), follow `next_url` cursor (≤50 pages × 1000 rows), group locally by `tickers[0]` (primary-issuer attribution). Live-probe 2026-06-09 confirmed entitlement on the no-ticker variant. Orchestrator restructured to run the existing `filterQualifyingTransactions` per-universe-ticker BEFORE any per-ticker market-cap fetch — tickers with 0 qualifying rows skip `no_qualifying_transactions` without any shares/price HTTPS call. Compute / filter / classifier / z-score / persist BYTE-UNCHANGED. New `PolygonForm4Fetcher.fetchForm4MarketWide` + `Form4Row.tickers?: string[]` + `Form4MarketWideResult` type. The per-ticker `fetchForm4` is retained for the manual single-ticker debug path. +6 fetcher tests (single-page grouping, next_url pagination, 403/404 typed-missing, missing-tickers drop, multi-ticker tickers[0] attribution); 12 orchestrator tests preserved BYTE-IDENTICAL through the mock-shape change. ESLint 0 errors on the 4 changed files; no `any`; no `eslint-disable`; no `Date.now()`; div-by-zero guard preserved. Authority: ACT-155. Pattern becomes the template for the remaining feed-signals (#1 / #2 / #3 / #8) — any per-ticker-across-the-universe fetch would hit the same CPU wall.

**Second addendum (ACT-156, 2026-06-09) — Signal #4 disarmed; Polygon Form 4 endpoint silently ignores filters (INC-70); P2 double-tally fix; EDGAR rebuild deferred (DW-094). Constitution Rule 8 supersession via addendum; original entry + ACT-155 addendum preserved verbatim.**

After the ACT-155 market-wide rewrite, the next fire returned HTTP 200 but with the impossible telemetry `fetch_error: 839` AND `no_qualifying_transactions: 839` simultaneously (1678 skips for 839-ticker universe; `signal_compute_log.run_id='1021808b-4b1c-4b04-bb45-d989d56b5193'`). Investigation surfaced TWO distinct problems:

**P1 (INC-70 — data correctness, severity HIGH).** A 9-variant probe of `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4` against the deployed `POLYGON_API_KEY` returned **byte-identical 1.85 MB / 1000-row firehose responses** for: single-ticker, `ticker.any_of`, comma-list, `ticker.in`, pipe-delimited, repeated-param, market-wide 90-day, AND market-wide 14-day. `ticker=AAPL` returned rows NOT containing AAPL (top buckets: DELL=312, FCNCA=41, SSMR=38). Only `limit=` was honored. The endpoint silently ignores `ticker` (every syntax) AND `transaction_date.*` — Candidates A (multi-ticker), B (date-chunked), C (per-ticker) are ALL impossible at this entitlement tier. The original FP-042 per-ticker fetcher was therefore broken since deploy: it fetched the same firehose 839×, locally filtered by `tickers[0]===ticker` (would yield ~0 rows per name since our universe isn't dominant in the firehose), and the compute layer marked each ticker `no_qualifying_transactions` — perfectly indistinguishable from the EXPECTED sparse profile. **Phantom signal: looked sparse, was actually wrong.** The ACT-155 CPU failure was the lucky trigger that exposed the silent data bug.

**P2 (telemetry double-tally).** The ACT-155 catch block pushed 839 per-ticker `fetch_error` skips on a market-wide throw, then assigned `marketWide = { kind:'rows', rowsByTicker: new Map() }` WITHOUT returning, falling through into the qualifying-filter loop which pushed 839 MORE `no_qualifying_transactions` skips. Two skip classes per ticker, 1678 total — telemetrically impossible.

**This addendum's scope.** (a) **`insider-orchestrator.ts`** — the market-wide-throw catch block now returns `outcome='failed'` with `failure_reason='form4 market-wide fetch failed: <thrown message>'` and `skipped: []`, short-circuiting before the qualifying-filter loop. A run can never again show two skip classes for the same ticker. The `kind:'unavailable'` branch (typed entitlement state — 403/404 — neither a throw nor a fetcher error) is UNCHANGED. (b) **`signal_registry`** — `insider_transactions_90d.status` flipped `'live' → 'planned'` via data UPDATE (no new MIG); `job_registry.longshort.insider.compute.enabled` stays `false`. The non-critical signal rule (§6.5) means the combiner imputes Signal #4's absence and the long-short stack continues to rank on momentum / reversal / short-interest. (c) **FP-042 compute / classifier / filter / z-score code is UNTOUCHED** — it is correct and reused as-is when the EDGAR fetcher lands (DW-094). Only the data-acquisition layer needs replacing. (d) **Docs** — INC-70, DW-094, this addendum, ACT-156, `insider-transactions.md` ACT-156 addendum, NEW `_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md`.

**Pattern lesson (codified, applies to remaining feed-signals).** Every vendor-endpoint fetcher MUST implement a `verifyFilterHonored()` pre-flight: probe with a filter value that SHOULD return zero rows (e.g. `ticker=ZZZZZZZZ`, far-future date) and assert the response is empty — NOT a firehose. If a should-be-empty query returns data, the filter is silently ignored and client-side filtering will produce wrong data. This would have caught INC-70 on day one. Bound as a §22.3-style pre-flight item for Signals #1 (analyst), #2 (PEAD), #3 (options), #8 (news) — each has its own vendor endpoint whose filters must be probed before trust. Documented at `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md`.

**Evidence.** Gate 4 ESLint on changed files — 0 errors, 0 warnings. Gate 4 Vitest — `50 files / 388 tests passed`. Gate 11 Deno scoped to FP-042 surfaces — `70 passed / 0 failed`. Live-DB §22.5.1 confirms `signal_registry.status='planned'` and `job_registry.enabled=false`. **Original FP-042 entry + ACT-155 addendum preserved verbatim per Constitution Rule 8.** Forward-pointer: INC-70 + DW-094 + ACT-156.

#### FP-041 Addendum (revision-fix) — `si_pct_float` derivation (Option A)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Post-execution manual validation (operator) returned `outcome=completed`, `universe_size=839`, `persisted_count=0`, `insufficient_history=839`, all other skip counts = 0 — entitlement + network + history were fine, but every ticker normalized to zero reports. Read-only investigation traced the cause to `polygon-short-interest-fetcher.ts` `normalizeRow` requiring a `short_percent_of_float` field that Polygon's `/stocks/v1/short-interest` endpoint **does not return**; sample-response field set (verified against live docs) is `ticker / settlement_date / short_interest / avg_daily_volume / days_to_cover`. The anti-phantom guard correctly refused to fabricate a percentage and the failure surfaced cleanly as `insufficient_history` instead of producing garbage z-scores. |
| **Decision** | Option A — derive `si_pct_float = short_interest / share_class_shares_outstanding`, preserving the spec §4.4.3 %-of-float denomination. Option B (`days_to_cover` substitution) was REJECTED — it deviates from §4.4.3 and is inconsistent with §3.3e (also % -of-float) and would require a fresh DEC. The shares-outstanding side input is already available from the SAME Polygon reference endpoint that universe-enrichment uses (`/v3/reference/tickers/{ticker}` returns `share_class_shares_outstanding`); NO new subscription entitlement is introduced. |
| **Conscious approximation (documented per §2 axiom 4)** | The reference endpoint provides ONLY CURRENT shares-outstanding; the signal compares SI at two HISTORICAL report dates (T and T-2 ≈ 30 calendar days). We denominate BOTH historical SI counts with current shares-outstanding. Defensible because shares-outstanding is slow-moving (corporate actions on the order of quarters/years) relative to SI swings (twice-monthly, often orders-of-magnitude); the percentage change is dominated by the SI numerator change. Point-in-time shares-outstanding history (which would account for splits / buybacks shifting the denominator) is not exposed by this endpoint and would require a FINRA + EDGAR cross-source (same backup roadmap as the SI fetcher; intentionally deferred). The approximation is pinned in three places by design: orchestrator file-level comment, `function-index.md` orchestrator row, and `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/short-interest-change.md`. |
| **Scope** | NEW `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-shares-outstanding-fetcher.ts` (+ `_test.ts`; 10 tests including missing/zero/negative/NaN → typed-absence; entitlement-aware). EDIT `polygon-short-interest-fetcher.ts` — return shape now `{ report_date, short_interest }` (raw share count); normalizeRow requires finite `short_interest >= 0` and drops missing/negative; zero is VALID (genuinely no shorts). EDIT `short-interest-orchestrator.ts` — context now includes `sharesOutstanding: PolygonSharesOutstandingFetcher`; parallel `Promise.all` fetch per ticker; map raw reports to `ShortInterestReport { si_pct_float }` via derivation; new `missing_shares_outstanding` skip when denominator unavailable; defensive zero-shares re-check inside orchestrator (orchestrator catches divide-by-zero even if fetcher's guard regresses). EDIT `signal-types.ts` — add `missing_shares_outstanding` to `SignalSkipReason` (now 7 members). EDIT `_shared/persist-signal-compute-log.ts` — seed `missing_shares_outstanding: 0` in `aggregateSkipCounts`. EDIT `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` — 3 exact-match-assertion updates (stale-test lesson applied). EDIT both handlers (`longshort-short-interest-compute{,-manual}/index.ts`) — construct `PolygonSharesOutstandingFetcher` and pass into ctx (KEEP-IN-SYNC pair). EDIT handler source-sentinel tests — assert `sharesOutstanding` field present. Docs same-PR: this addendum; `function-index.md` (NEW shares-outstanding fetcher entry; UPDATE short-interest fetcher entry + orchestrator entry with derivation + conscious-approximation note + 7-member enum); `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/short-interest-change.md` (denominator-derivation paragraph + new dataflow diagram + new skip-reason row + dedicated "Conscious approximation" section); ACT-153a (this revision-fix). NO new permissions / routes / migrations / npm dependencies / env-vars. |
| **Anti-pattern guards (Tier A)** | (1) NO fabricated denominator — missing shares → typed `missing_shares_outstanding` skip; (2) DIVIDE-BY-ZERO guard at TWO layers — fetcher rejects zero/negative/NaN/Infinity shares (8 dedicated tests), orchestrator defensively re-checks (test (17) lying-fetcher mock proves it); (3) the spec formula `-1 × (SI%[T] − SI%[T-2])` is preserved — only the SOURCE of `si_pct_float` changes from "phantom field read" to "raw count divided by shares"; (4) NO wall-clock drift — orchestrator timestamps still derive from `as_of`; (5) the conscious-approximation comment is mandatory (the approximation must be VISIBLE — pinned in 3 places). |
| **Verification** | Gate 11 (Deno) scoped to FP-041 + affected shared files: **77 passed / 0 failed** (`polygon-short-interest-fetcher_test.ts` 10/10, `polygon-shares-outstanding-fetcher_test.ts` 10/10, `compute-short-interest_test.ts` 13/13, `short-interest-orchestrator_test.ts` 17/17 — incl. 5 new derivation-path tests, `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` 9/9, `longshort-short-interest-compute/index_test.ts` 9/9, `longshort-short-interest-compute-manual/index_test.ts` 9/9). Full Gate 11 (excluding 3 pre-existing broken universe test files unrelated to FP-041): **568 passed / 11 failed** (all 11 failures are pre-existing profile/user-management integration tests requiring a live test Supabase, unrelated to FP-041 — same failures on main). Gate 4 Vitest: **388 passed / 50 files**. Gate 4 ESLint scoped to changed FP-041 files: **0 warnings / 0 errors** (the 15 pre-existing warnings are all in unrelated files on main). No `any`. |

### FP-045 Phase 4 Pacing Revision-Fix Addendum (Rule 8 — Forward-Pointer, Original Preserved)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Addendum Date** | 2026-06-10 |
| **Authority** | Operator REVISION-FIX mode ("Pacing reconciliation (the blocker)") following the Phase 4 closure addendum above. |
| **Defect** | The Phase 4 closure pinned `callsPerName=1` in `options-flow-queue-registration.ts` while the `TradierOptionsChainFetcher` per-ticker arm makes TWO wire calls (`/markets/options/expirations` + `/markets/options/chains`). The closure addendum's arithmetic row computed `(80 × 2) / 1.7 ≈ 94.1s` — citing the TRUE wire-call count — but the registration carried `callsPerName=1` and the adapter wrapped HTTP in a SECOND `pacedHttpFetch`-bound `TokenBucket(ratePerSec=1.7)`. Two buckets at the same rate, serialized: per-ticker time = worker-bucket(1/1.7=0.59s) + adapter-bucket(2/1.7=1.18s) ≈ 1.76s; 80-slice ≈ 141s, dangerously close to the 150s HTTP wall and inconsistent with the documented ≈94.1s budget. The drift sentinel test masked the defect by hand-entering `(sliceSize * 2)` instead of deriving from `callsPerName`. |
| **Trace evidence** | (1) `TradierOptionsChainFetcher.fetchChainForTicker` — one `/markets/options/expirations` call + one `/markets/options/chains` call per ticker = 2 wire calls (file: `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/tradier-options-chain-fetcher.ts`). (2) Pre-fix `options-flow-queue-registration.ts:88-91` constructed `new TokenBucket({ratePerSec:1.7})` + `pacedHttpFetch(bucket, fetch)` and passed `paced` to `TradierOptionsChainFetcher`. (3) `queue-slice-worker.ts:122-133` constructs its own `TokenBucket(ratePerSec=cfg.ratePerSec)` via `defaultBucketFactory` and acquires `cfg.callsPerName` tokens per ticker BEFORE invoking `fetchAndCompute`. (4) Two independent buckets → double-acquisition; TRUE tokens against Tradier wire = 2/ticker via adapter bucket; TRUE effective Tradier wire rate ≈ 1.7 rps (102/min < 120/min cap — never breached) but TRUE per-ticker wall time ≈ 1.76s, not the documented 1.18s. |
| **Fix** | Pacing collapsed to ONE place — the slice-worker bucket (engine-owned). (1) `options-flow-queue-registration.ts` — removed `TokenBucket` + `pacedHttpFetch` import; adapter receives raw `fetch`; `callsPerName` flipped `1 → 2` to match the fetcher's true wire-call count. (2) `options-flow-queue-registration_test.ts` — introduced `TRADIER_WIRE_CALLS_PER_TICKER = 2` constant; arithmetic-budget test now asserts `callsPerName === TRADIER_WIRE_CALLS_PER_TICKER` AND derives `perSliceWire = (sliceSize × callsPerName) / ratePerSec` (no more hand-entered `* 2`); 94.1s pin retained. (3) `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/queue-worker.md` — arithmetic table row + Phase-4 consumer table updated to `callsPerName=2`; Pacing contract section adds the single-bucket invariant ("adapters MUST pass raw `fetch`"). (4) DW-095 closure cell + this addendum carry the corrected text. |
| **Burst-vs-smooth verification** | With `callsPerName=2`, worker acquires both tokens UPFRONT (`for (let i=0; i<callsPerName; i++) await bucket.acquire()`) then invokes the adapter which makes two HTTP calls back-to-back. Wire-pattern: 2 calls every 1.18s = 1.7 rps average. Tradier's 120/min cap is a minute-window cap (not strict per-second), so the burst-of-2 shape is well-tolerated. The slice cron handler runs synchronously inside `Deno.serve` (`longshort-queue-slice/index.ts`); 94.1s slice + ≤5s finalizer (only on the CAS-winning final slice) ≈ 99s wall, comfortably under the 150s HTTP wall — no `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` needed, no job-timeout column to raise. |
| **DEC-047 status confirmation** | DEC-047 already carries `Status: implemented (FP-045 generalized engine + Phase 3 PEAD consumer + Phase 4 options-flow consumer; DW-095 closed)` in `docs/08-planning/approved-decisions.md` (grep-verified). No re-flip needed. |
| **MIG-085 §22.5.1 live evidence** | Post-fix live-DB read (2026-06-10): `signal_registry.options_flow_imbalance_5d` → `status='live'`, `cadence='daily (after-close; queue-drained ~11 min; interim per DEC-048 — §4.4.7 5-min intraday target deferred per DEC-046 v2)'`, `planned_phase=NULL`. `job_registry.longshort.options_flow.compute` → `enabled=false`, `status='registered'`, `schedule='0 22 * * 1-5'`, `handler_path='supabase/functions/longshort-options-flow-compute/index.ts'`, description carries the FP-045 Phase 4 queue-shim shape. Both rows preserved per FP-045 §5; MIG-085 metadata-only flip stands. |
| **Gate evidence** | Gate 2 (wall-clock scanner): CLEAN (0 violations on changed files). Gate 4 (CI ESLint command verbatim): `npx eslint .` — 0 errors / 15 baseline warnings. Gate 11 (Deno tests): `options-flow-queue-registration_test.ts` 6 tests green including the new structural assertion `callsPerName === TRADIER_WIRE_CALLS_PER_TICKER`. |
| **Failure-mode escalation** | Promoted to Catalog #39 — "Vendor-cap arithmetic inconsistency between documented budget and code-level config" — per the §12.10 operational-log entry below ("SECOND occurrence" trigger; first firing FP-044 Phase 2 PEAD orchestrator, second firing FP-045 Phase 4 options-flow registration). Codification target: the structural test pattern landed in `options-flow-queue-registration_test.ts` (drift sentinel must derive the arithmetic row from the FETCHER's actual wire-call count, not a hand-entered constant) is the prevention shape; future per-signal registrations MUST follow it. |

### FP-039: Cron-Auth Outage Remediation (atomic env-rotate + redeploy + command-reconcile across 6 consumers)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-039 (FP-038 reserved for signal-registry spec — NOT consumed here; highest live prior FP at HEAD is FP-037). |
| **Status** | Applied + verified (operator-run out-of-band, 2026-06-08). Bucket-C-unblocking. Supersedes FP-019 as a standalone fix (FP-019 was partial — see FP-019 Addendum below). |
| **Problem** | Every cron consumer (jobids 34/35/36/37/48/51) returned 401 Unauthorized on every fire; `cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'` concealed the failure. Root cause: three concurrent defects — divergent secret values across cron commands (5 jobs on `ee867b97…`, jobid:48 on `076426…`), deployed `CRON_SECRET` env var matching neither, and INC-63 redeploy-required constraint (env binds at deploy). FP-019's partial corrective aligned 5 command-sides but missed jobid:48 and skipped the env+redeploy step — net effect was 0% cron auth success until FP-039. See INC-69 for full surface story. |
| **Resolution (operator-run, applied + verified 2026-06-08)** | One atomic remediation: (1) generated a new canonical `CRON_SECRET` value; (2) updated the Supabase Edge Function secret `CRON_SECRET` to that value; (3) redeployed all 6 consumers so they bind the new env at runtime (`longshort-momentum-compute`, `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh`, `job-health-check`, `job-alert-evaluation`, `job-metrics-aggregate`, `job-audit-cleanup`); (4) reconciled all 6 cron commands via `cron.schedule(...)` idempotent upserts so every `X-Cron-Secret` header carries the canonical value. Per §22.5.3 plaintext-secret out-of-band path: no MIG allocated. |
| **End-to-end evidence (DEC-043 standard)** | (a) Platform jobs 34/35 returned **200 `{"state":"succeeded","success":true}`** on schedule 2026-06-08 10:02–11:58 UTC (first 200s in the retention window). (b) Momentum jobid:51 test-fired and produced **two cron-attributable `signal_compute_log` rows** — `completed_at` 2026-06-08 11:52:00 and 11:54:00 UTC, `as_of_date` 2026-06-08, `outcome=completed`, `persisted_count=834` (real wall-clock; not the midnight-manual signature). (c) Byte-match attestation for jobid:51: exactly 1 `cron.job` row, `schedule = '0 20 * * 1-5'` byte-identical to `job_registry.schedule`, `active=true`, command carries resolved project ref + real secret (no placeholder literals). |
| **Scope (atomic, all-or-nothing across 6 consumers)** | Env-var rotate + Edge Function redeploy ×6 + `cron.schedule()` reconcile ×6 in one operator session. Treating this as 6 separate fixes would have left a partial-success state similar to FP-019. |
| **Out of Scope** | Warmup jobs 29-33 (405s — pre-existing, separate INC when addressed). CRON_SECRET rotation to a chat-clean value (deferred to DW-091, which is updated-not-closed — see DW-091 addendum). HEAD c443000 reconciliation (unrelated). Any code/migration/schema/RLS change. |
| **Reference Impact** | `docs/06-tracking/incidental-findings.md` (INC-69); `docs/08-planning/approved-decisions.md` (DEC-043); `docs/00-governance/definition-of-done.md` (new DoD checklist item referencing DEC-043); `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md` (ACT-150 + ACT-130); `docs/08-planning/deferred-work-register.md` (DW-091 addendum row); `sql/14_longshort_signal_cron_schedule.sql` (header-comment correction — canonical live-verified pattern is now jobid:51, NOT jobid:48). No new permissions, events, configs, env-vars, migrations, routes, edge functions, shared helpers, or npm deps. No MIG (operator-run plaintext-secret out-of-band per §22.5.3). |
| **Decision ID** | DEC-043 (the new scheduled-job-attestation rule motivated by INC-69 — verbatim codification of "enabled ≠ scheduled ≠ authenticated; attestation requires end-to-end 200 + real artifact row"). |
| **Reviewed By** | Operator |
| **Review Date** | 2026-06-08 |

**Closure** — Applied + verified out-of-band by operator 2026-06-08. The 6-consumer cron-auth path is restored end-to-end; first cron-attributable 200s + artifact rows in the project's retention history landed at 10:02–11:58 + 11:52/11:54 UTC. FP-018 Bucket C now closeable per ACT-130 on stronger evidence than originally planned. Forward-binding: DEC-043 is the binding attestation standard going forward; DW-091 remains open with a hard tripwire to close before Phase 8 (small live capital).

Authority: ACT-150 (FP-039/INC-69 governance authoring) + ACT-130 (FP-018 Bucket C closure).

### FP-056: Phase-5 Paper-Exec Execution Layer — Sequential Submitter + Two-Phase State Machine + Autonomous Three-Tier Resolution (longshort)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-056 (next-free after FP-055). |
| **Status** | **CHARTERED 2026-06-24 at ACT-305** — governance-only authoring (this entry + [DEC-068](../decisions/DEC-068-longshort-execution-authorization.md)). NO code, NO submitter, NO `longshort.execute` permission seed at the charter PR (per DEC-032 clause (4) — key only when code exists; this charter authorizes the introduction TRIGGER, the E5 build performs the introduction). E1–E6 build is the next prompt sequence. |
| **Tier** | **A — financial-critical money path.** This is the FP that places paper orders against the live combiner book. The autonomous-resolution clause + the fallback-as-execution-input clause are load-bearing operator acceptances (see DEC-068 clauses (a) + (b)). |
| **Authority chain** | Operator-ratified DEC-068 (this commit) — investigation (Lovable, charter scoping + DW-138 reframe + §8.9 schema-fit grep) + reconciliation (supervisor, v1 cut + fallback-as-exec-input + spec-anchor verification at §8.6.1 line 109 + §8.6.2 line 187) + **operator amendment** (autonomous three-tier resolution; operator paging reserved for invariant violations only — RATIFICATION of existing spec posture, NOT novel philosophy) + supervisor re-verification (the amendment realigns the charter with spec). Charter-first sequencing (inverse of DEC-067 which was built-then-authored) because the DEC AUTHORIZES the money path the build performs (DEC-032 clause (4)). |
| **Problem** | FP-055 (Step A, ACT-302) writes per-ticker target rows to `longshort_target_positions` and emits `longshort.targets.published` — the sizing → execution trigger surface. **Nothing consumes that trigger.** DW-046 is the registered execution-path work-item; DW-047 is the registered `longshort.execute` permission key; DEC-036 clause (4) reserves the key against the FP that introduces execution code; ADR-002 locks v1 placement to sequential. The execution layer is the actual time-to-paper-trading constraint — sizing is done, the cron+combiner+book pipeline is live, the only missing piece is the loop that turns target rows into broker calls under bounded, autonomous correctness discipline. |
| **Resolution (v1 cut — minimal coherent paper-order-placement loop)** | **v1.a Delta-computer** (pure compute) — reads target rows + current Alpaca positions, computes `delta = target_qty - current_qty` per ticker; emits a `noop` for held targets matching qty (§1.5 "existing positions are not disturbed" rule). **v1.b §7 pre-flight gate composer** — wraps the existing verify_* surfaces (`verify_halt_status`, `verify_short_availability`, `verify_ssr_status`, `verify_buying_power`, `verify_position`) into a single gate-composer that runs BEFORE every submit attempt; the §7 gates are the PRIMARY defense for pause-class causes (routing correctness is the system's responsibility, not the broker's). **v1.c Sequential submitter** — `POST /v2/orders` one at a time via `AlpacaPaperClient.postJson` (the seam already exists); never two same-symbol same-side opposite-side in flight (ADR-002 wash-trade lock). Idempotency-key per target per tick (T8). **v1.d Two-phase state machine** — entry + rank-exit lifecycles per §8.6 (Acceptance → Fill); injected `Clock` (DEC-034 clause (4)). **v1.e §8.9 no-pause propagation** — handles `halted` / `htb` / transient `insufficient_buying_power` via cache-update + next-tick retry (autonomous, no operator). PAUSE classes (`ssr_violation` / `pdt_block` / persistent-BP) DEFERRED per DEC-068 clause (e). **v1.f `longshort.execute` permission key** — introduced at E5 build concurrent with the first consumer (DEC-032 clause (4); DEC-036 clause (4) retired per DEC-068 clause (d)). **v1.g Triple-evidence closure** — replay fixture + `reconciliation_events` telemetry + Alpaca paper spot-check (E6 DW-138-gated). **Autonomous three-tier resolution per DEC-068 clause (b):** Tier 1 auto-retry per §8.6.2 ladder bounded by `MAX_RETRY_ATTEMPTS=3` + `MAX_SLIPPAGE_BUDGET=50bps`; Tier 2 auto-skip terminal-for-tick with `unfillable_skip` state + re-eligible next tick (book operates one fewer name per §8.6.2 line 187); Tier 3 operator-page for invariant violations only. |
| **Build sub-ladder (E1–E6 — separate prompts)** | **E1 — `rebalance-planner` — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-307.** **E2 — `order-submitter` — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-310 (INC-77 closed same-PR).** **E3 — `state-machine` + `rejection-classifier` + `lifecycle-orchestrator` — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-311** (entry + rank_exit only; short-stop deferred to DW-149 — both halves; defensive STOP guard installed). **E4 — `cache-propagator` + `cache-propagator-io` + MIG-119 — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-312 (HYBRID resolution per DEC-068 clause e)** — operator-ratified after the §7-cache-existence probe found halted + transient-BP live verifiers are AUTHORITATIVE (a cache would INTRODUCE staleness per §2 axiom) while htb's prime ITSELF is stale (Alpaca `/v2/assets` shortable is a once-each-morning snapshot per Alpaca docs). Three NO-PAUSE classes: halted observability-emit ONLY (NO cache write — `failure_action='halt_cache_updated_from_rejection'` verbatim); transient-BP observability-emit ONLY (NO cache write — `failure_action='buying_power_cache_refreshed'` verbatim; the "refresh" is the next-tick live `verify_buying_power`); htb persisted-state record via MIG-119 `longshort_short_availability_cache` (`failure_action='short_availability_cache_updated_htb'` verbatim) — UPSERT on rejection, DELETE on genuine-success locate (`qty_available >= qty_requested`), partial locate (`qty_available < qty_requested`) does NOT clear. Inline E3→E4 seam: `lifecycle-orchestrator` invokes propagator synchronously in the rejection branch (zero propagation lag). The LOAD-BEARING verifier-side wiring: `verify_short_availability` consults the htb cache as a fast-fail gate BEFORE the broker locate call (without the consult, the record is written but never read and the loop is NOT broken). 34 new tests + 209/209 longshort-execution + longshort-verifiers suite green; §22.5.1 live-DB round-trip verified (clear-on-genuine-success works; partial does not clear). PAUSE classes (ssr_violation / pdt_block / persistent-BP 3-in-1h) registered as DW-150 / DW-151 / DW-152 — they continue to terminalize tier-3 via E3's rejection-classifier so the safety posture is unchanged; only the cache propagation + operator-pause routing are deferred. **E5 — `longshort.execute` permission seed (MIG-120) + `longshort-execute` edge fn (tick-scheduler envelope over E3 `advanceTick`) + RBAC gate — LANDED 2026-06-24 at ACT-313.** Co-equal-PR introduction satisfies DEC-032 clause 4 (key + first consumer same PR per DEC-068 clause d). NO default role grants (DEC-031 sub-point 10 — granting is a separate operator action; superadmin inherits via wildcard). The edge fn gates on `checkPermissionOrThrow(authCtx.user.id, 'longshort.execute')` IMMEDIATELY after `authenticateRequest` and BEFORE any broker-facing call; the live broker factory deliberately THROWS `LiveBrokerNotProvisionedError` until DW-138 + E6 wires `AlpacaPaperClient` (DEC-034 clause 3 — NO swallow + phantom-success; the throw maps to a 503 envelope via `_shared/handler.ts`). Composer↔verifier layering: classification rules transcribed VERBATIM from the `verify_*.ts` specs (`verify_halt_status` halted=true → fail; `verify_ssr_status` state≠not_active → fail; `verify_short_availability` !available OR qty_available<1 → fail; `verify_buying_power` available_bp<requested_total → ALL candidates fail with system-level reason). The composer DOES NOT call `reconcile()` — pre-flight gates run O(candidates × verifiers) per tick (up to 160 calls per rebalance) and would FLOOD `reconciliation_events` with PASS rows; the standalone `verify_*.ts` shells remain the AUTHORITATIVE reconciliation surface for post-fill / sweep contexts. Per §2 axiom (TYPED ABSENCE, NOT a synthetic SSR-clear sentinel) the composer accepts `ssrStatusFetcher?` as OPTIONAL — when absent, every SHORT candidate records `verifiers_skipped: ['verify_ssr_status']` in the composer's `skipped` Map AND the batch `summary.ssr_unavailable=true`. The candidate's `PreflightResult` reflects ONLY the verifiers that actually RAN — the composer does NOT synthesize an SSR-clear result, and it does NOT fabricate a failure where no signal exists. LONG candidates are unaffected (verify_ssr_status is short-side-only routing concern). **Phase-2 decision (operator)**: (a) inject an SSR fetcher from a non-Alpaca feed (Polygon / Tradier); (b) accept the degraded posture on paper v1 and document the typed absence in the operator runbook + the trigger's audit metadata; (c) defer short-side placement entirely until an SSR source is wired (long-only paper v1). Recommendation lands with the Phase-2 trigger build. The composer is structured to accept (a) without code change — Phase-2 just injects the fetcher into the trigger. |

### FP-055: Step A — Portfolio Construction / Target-Position Compute (longshort)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **ID** | FP-055 (next-free after FP-054). |
| **Status** | approved (operator-directed forward 2026-06-24 — Tier A; greenfield pure-compute upstream half of the paper-trading pipeline; ZERO execution surface, ZERO `longshort.execute` introduction, ZERO broker write, ZERO order). |
| **Problem** | The fallback combiner book lands daily (20L + 20S in `combiner_book`) but nothing downstream sizes it. The execution layer (DW-046) is DEC-gated and blocked until a sizing DEC ratifies (a) the binding of CROSSWIND §1.5 "current invested capital" to a broker field, (b) the per-name fraction, (c) partial-book policy, and (d) the v1 sizing model. The DEC needs OBSERVED numbers against the live book to lock those parameters — which requires the sizing kernel to exist FIRST as a pure-compute layer. |
| **Resolution** | Build a pure-compute portfolio-construction layer that reads `combiner_book` + `BrokerBuyingPower.account_equity` and writes per-ticker target-state rows to a new `longshort_target_positions` table. NO broker write. NO order. NO `longshort.execute`. Sizing kernel uses two named params: `allocation_pct` ∈ (0, 1] (D4, default 1.0, dashboard-settable) and `leverage` (D5, paper-locked at 1.0; Phase-8 DEC DW-137 unlocks). Math: `capital_base = equity × allocation_pct × leverage`; `per_name_notional = capital_base / book_size`. At full book (40) / allocation=1.0 / leverage=1.0 each name gets `equity / 40` (matches §1.5 verbatim — 2.5% per name, 100% gross, dollar-neutral). Decoupled `longshort.targets.published` audit event emitted after each successful UPSERT — the Step F sizing→execution trigger surface (analogous to `combiner.book_published`). |
| **Scope** | NEW: `supabase/migrations/…_longshort_target_positions.sql` (MIG-118) — table + RLS + GRANTs mirroring `combiner_book` verbatim; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-targets/{target-position-builder,target-position-orchestrator,stub-capital-fetcher}.ts` + `…/target-position-builder_test.ts` (15 tests); `supabase/functions/longshort-targets-compute/index.ts` + `…-manual/index.ts`. EDIT: `docs/07-reference/{event-index.md,function-index.md,database-migration-ledger.md}`, `docs/08-planning/{feature-proposals.md,deferred-work-register.md}`, `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md`. |
